Appendix C
Progress

C.1
Overview

The progress proof requires several steps. We overview them below.

Canonical forms

Canonical forms allow us to reason globally about a configuration by putting it in a structured form. Lemma
31
states that any well-typed, closed configuration can be put into canonical form.

Exception-aware runtime typing environments

Runtime typing environments do not account explicitly for zapper threads.
This makes sense when analysing protocols statically to check their safety
and progress properties, but is inconvenient when reasoning about
configurations.

The second step is to introduce
exception-aware
runtime typing
environments which account explicitly for zapper threads and the propagation
of exceptions, and an exception-aware runtime typing
system (
32
). We then show that configurations including
zapper threads are typable under the exception-aware runtime typing
system (
36
).

We refine our notion of environment progress to include the possibility of
failed sessions (
39
), and show that given a runtime environment

\Delta
satisfying safety and progress, a derived exception-aware runtime
environment
\Theta
also satisfies safety and
progress (
40
).

Flattenings

Hu & Yoshida’s formulation of multiparty session types relaxes the
directedness constraint for output choices, but this relaxation makes it
more difficult to reason about reduction of environments being reflected by
configurations.

In this step, we introduce
flattenings

(Definitions
43

and
44
),
which restict each top-level output choice to a single option, and show that
if a configuration is
ready
(i.e., all actors are blocked on
communication actions), then it is typable under a flattened
environment (
46
). We then show that flattenings preserve
environment reducibility (
47
).

Session progress

The penultimate step is to show that sessions can make progress.
A key result is
49
, which states that a ready
configuration typable under a flat exception-aware typing environment can
reduce.
The session progress lemma (
18
) follows
from
49
and the previous results.

Progress

Finally, progress follows from
18
and case analysis on the
disconnected actors.

C.2
Auxiliary Definitions

Let
\Psi
range over typing environments containing only runtime names:
\Psi::=\cdot\,\mid\,\Psi,a:{\textsf{Pid}}(S)
.

Term reduction satisfies a form of progress: a well-typed term is a value, can
reduce, or is a communication or concurrency construct:

Lemma 28

(Progress (terms))
.

If
\{T\}\>\>\Psi\,\mid\,{S}\triangleright{M}{:}{A}\triangleleft{S^{\prime}}
, then either
M={\textbf{{return}}}\>V
for some value
V
; there exists some
N
such that
M\longrightarrow_{\mathsf{M}}N
; or there exists some
E

such that
M
can be written
E[N]
for some
E,N
where
N
is either

raise
or an adaptation, communication, or concurrency construct.

Lemma 29

(Session typability)
.

If
\cdot;\cdot\vdash\mathcal{G}[\mathcal{S}]
, then there exist
\Psi,\Delta

such that

\Psi;\Delta\vdash\mathcal{S}
and

\Delta
only consists of entries of the form
a:S
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
\mathcal{G}
, noting that entries of the
form
s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}\rangle{:}S
are linear;
by the definition of
\mathcal{S}=(\nu s)\mathcal{C}
, actors and zapper threads
in
\mathcal{C}
must refer to only to
s
.
∎

C.3
Canonical forms

To help us state a progress property, we consider configurations in

canonical form
.

A canonical form consists of binders for all connected actor names, followed by
binders for all disconnected actor names, followed by all sessions,
followed by all disconnected actors.

Definition 30

(Canonical form)
.

A configuration is in
canonical form
if it is either
\mathbf{0}
or can be written:

(\nu a_{1}\cdots a_{l})(\nu b_{1}\cdots b_{m})(\mathcal{S}_{1}\parallel\cdots\parallel\mathcal{S}_{n}\parallel\langle b_{1},M_{1},\bot,\kappa_{1}\rangle\parallel\cdots\parallel\langle b_{m},M_{m},\bot,\kappa_{n}\rangle)
.

Every well-typed, closed configuration can be written in canonical form.

Lemma 31

(Canonical forms)
.

If
\cdot;\cdot\vdash\mathcal{C}
, then
\exists\mathcal{D}\equiv\mathcal{C}
where
\mathcal{D}
is in canonical form.

Proof sketch.

Due to Lemma
23
, by induction on typing derivations we can
move all actor name restrictions to the top level, followed by all session
name restrictions, followed by all connected actors, followed by all zapper
threads, followed by all disconnected actors.

Since the typing rules ensure each actor only participates in a single
session, we can then group each actor and zapper thread according to its
session, in order to arrive at a canonical form.
∎

C.4
Exception-aware runtime typing environments

Due to
E-RaiseP
, zapper threads expose additional reductions to those
allowed by standard environment reduction. In particular, given the presence of
a zapper thread
\lightning s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]
, any other participant in the remainder of
the session blocked on
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}
can reduce. In order to account for this, we
introduce the notion of exception-aware runtime typing environments, environment
reduction, and runtime typing.

Definition 32

(Exception-aware runtime typing environments, environment
reduction, and runtime typing)
.

An
exception-aware runtime typing environment

\Theta
is defined as
follows:

\begin{array}[]{lrcl}\Theta&::=&a:S\,\mid\,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}\rangle{:}S\,\mid\,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]:\lightning\end{array}

The
exception-aware runtime typing relation

\Gamma;\Theta\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{C}
is defined to be the standard runtime
typing relation
\Gamma;\Delta\vdash\mathcal{C}
but with rule

T-Zap
defined as:

\frac{}{\Gamma;s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]:\lightning\vdash_{\lightning}\lightning s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]}\quad\textsc{T-Zap}

We extend the set of labels
\rho
with a zapper label

s:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\lightning{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}
, which states that role
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}
has
attempted to interact with a cancelled role
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}
and has become cancelled
itself as a result.

The
exception-aware environment reduction relation
is defined as the
rules of the standard runtime typing relation
\Delta\Longrightarrow\Delta^{\prime}
with the
addition of the following rules:

\frac{\exists j\in I.S_{j}={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{\dagger}{\ell_{j}(A_{j})}\,{.}\,S^{\prime}_{j}}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]:\lightning,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}\rangle{:}\Sigma_{i\in I}(S_{i})\xRightarrow{s:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\lightning{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]:\lightning,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]:\lightning}\quad\textsc{ET-CommFail}\par\frac{}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]:\lightning,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]\langle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\rangle{:}{\#}{\downarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}\xRightarrow{s:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\lightning{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]:\lightning,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]:\lightning}\quad\textsc{ET-DisconnFail}\par\frac{}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]:\lightning,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]\langle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\rangle{:}{\#}{\uparrow}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}\,{.}\,S\xRightarrow{s:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\lightning{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]:\lightning,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]:\lightning}\quad\textsc{ET-WaitFail}

Definition 33

(Zapped roles)
.

Define the
zapped roles
of session

\mathcal{S}=(\nu s)(\langle a_{1},M_{1},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{1}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}_{1}\rangle,\kappa_{1}\rangle\parallel\cdots\parallel\langle a_{m},M_{m},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{m}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}_{m}\rangle,\kappa_{m}\rangle\parallel{\rm ZAp}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{m+1}]}\parallel\cdots\parallel{\rm ZAp}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{n}]})

as
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{m+1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{n}
.

Definition 34

(Zapped environment)
.

Given a runtime environment

\Delta=a_{1}:S_{1},\ldots,a_{l}:S_{l},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{1}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}_{1}\rangle{:}T_{1},\ldots s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{m}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}_{m}\rangle{:}T_{m},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}_{1}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}_{1}\rangle{:}T^{\prime}_{1},\ldots s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}_{n}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}_{n}\rangle{:}T^{\prime}_{n}

and a set of roles
\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}_{1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}_{n}
,
the
zapped environment

\Theta={\textsf{zap}}(\Delta,\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}})
is defined
as:

\Delta=a_{1}:S_{1},\ldots,a_{l}:S_{l},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{1}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}_{1}\rangle{:}T_{1},\ldots s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{m}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}_{m}\rangle{:}T_{m},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}_{1}]:\lightning,\ldots s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}_{n}]:\lightning

Definition 35

(Failed environment)
.

An exception-aware runtime typing environment
\Theta
is a
failed
environent
, written
{\textsf{failed}}(\Theta)
, if
\Theta
is of the form
s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{1}]:\lightning,\ldots,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{n}]:\lightning
.

Lemma 36

(Zapped Typeability)
.

If
\Gamma;\Delta\vdash\mathcal{S}
where
\mathcal{S}=(\nu s:\Delta^{\prime})\mathcal{C}
, the zapped roles of
\mathcal{S}
are
\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}}
, and
\Theta={\textsf{zap}}(\Delta^{\prime},\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}})
, then

\Gamma;\Delta,\Theta\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

A straightforward induction on the derivation of

\Gamma;\Delta\vdash\mathcal{C}
, noting that the definition of sessions
and
T-Session
ensures that
\Delta
only contains actor
runtime names, and that the modified
T-Zap
rule is satisfied by the
fact that
\Theta={\textsf{zap}}(\Delta,\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}})
.
∎

Corollary 37
.

If
\cdot;\cdot\vdash\mathcal{C}
where
\mathcal{C}
is in canonical form,
then
\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Follows directly by applying Lemma
36
to each session.
∎

Corollary 38

(Exception-aware session typability)
.

If
\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{G}[\mathcal{S}]
, then there exist
\Psi,\Delta

such that
\Delta
only consists of entries of the form
a:S
and

\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{S}
.

Definition
13
extends straightforwardly to exception-aware runtime typing
environments.

Definition 39

(Progress (exception-aware environments))
.

An exception-aware runtime typing environment
\Theta

satisfies progress
, written

{\textsf{prog}}(\Theta)
, if:

•

(Role progress) for each
s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{i}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}_{i}\rangle{:}S_{i}\in\Theta
such that
{\textsf{active}}(S_{i})
, it is the case that
\Theta\longrightarrow^{*}\Theta^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\gamma}
with
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\in{\textsf{roles}}(\gamma)
.

•

(Eventual communication)
if
\Theta\Longrightarrow^{*}\Theta^{\prime}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{!}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{::}\ell(A)}
,
then either

\Theta^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\overrightarrow{\rho}}\Theta^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{?}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{::}\ell(A)}
,
or

\Theta^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\overrightarrow{\rho}}\Theta^{\prime\prime}\xRightarrow{s:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}\lightning{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}}
,
where
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\not\in{\textsf{roles}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})

•

(Correct termination)

\Theta\Longrightarrow^{*}\Theta^{\prime}\not\Longrightarrow
implies either

{\textsf{end}}(\Theta)
or
{\textsf{failed}}(\Theta)
.

If a runtime typing environment is deadlock-free, then any corresponding zapped
evironment will be deadlock-free.

Lemma 40

(Exception-aware environments preserve safety and progress)
.

Suppose
{\textsf{safe}}(\Delta)
and
{\textsf{prog}}(\Delta)
for some
\Theta
.

If
\Theta={\textsf{zap}}(\Delta,\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}})
for some set of roles
\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}}
,
then
{\textsf{safe}}(\Theta)
and
{\textsf{prog}}(\Theta)
.

Proof sketch.

Safety.

Safety follows since exception-aware environments do not modify session types, but
only replace them with zappers. Interaction with a zapper is vacuously safe,
so it follows that if
{\textsf{safe}}(\Delta)
then
{\textsf{safe}}(\Theta)
.

Progress.

For (role progress), we know that any active role in
\Delta
will eventually reduce.
Presence of a zapped role means that any other role in the session trying to
reduce with the zapped role will itself be zapped due to
ET-CommFail
,

ET-DisconnFail
, or
ET-WaitFail
; connections will not occur,
but this does not matter since the accepting role will not be in the session.

For (eventual communication),
we have that
if
\Delta\Longrightarrow^{*}\Delta^{\prime}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{!}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{::}\ell(A)}
,
then
\Delta^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\overrightarrow{\rho}}\Delta^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{?}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{::}\ell(A)}\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
where
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\not\in{\textsf{roles}}(\overrightarrow{\rho})
.

Suppose

\Theta\Longrightarrow^{*}\Theta^{\prime}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{!}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{::}\ell(A)}
.
If
\Theta^{\prime}
contains zapped roles, either they are irrelevant to
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}
’s reduction
and we can use the original reduction sequence to show

\Theta\Longrightarrow^{*}\Theta^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{?}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{::}\ell(A)}\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
or they are relevant to reduction and the exception propagates, resulting in

\Theta^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\overrightarrow{\rho}}\Theta^{\prime\prime}\xRightarrow{s:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}\lightning{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}}\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

For (correct termination), we know that
\Delta\not\Longrightarrow
implies that

{\textsf{end}}(\Delta)
. By (1), we know that each active role in
\Theta
will
eventually reduce, so it follows that either
{\textsf{end}}(\Theta)
(if all
communications occur successfully) or
{\textsf{failed}}(\Theta)
if a role has failed
and the failure has propagated to all other participants.

∎

C.5
Flattenings

Definition 41

(Input / Output-Directed Choices)
.

A choice session type
\Sigma_{i\in I}(\alpha_{i}\,{.}\,S_{i})
is a

output-directed
if each
\alpha_{i}
is either of the form

{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{i}}{!}{\ell_{i}}({A_{i}})
.

A choice session type is
input-directed
if it is of the form

\Sigma_{i\in I}({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{\dagger}{\ell_{i}(A_{i})})
for some

\dagger\in\{{?},{??}\}
.

For convenience, we write
S^{\bullet}
and
\Sigma^{\bullet}_{i\in I}(\alpha_{i}\,{.}\,S_{i})
to denote an output-directed choice, and
S^{\circ}
and

\Sigma^{\circ}_{i\in I}(\alpha_{i}\,{.}\,S_{i})
to denote an input-directed
choice.

Definition 42

(Output-flat session type)
.

An output-directed session type
\Sigma^{\bullet}_{i\in I}(S_{i}\,{.}\,S_{i})
is
output-flat
if it is a unary choice, i.e., can be written

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{!}{\ell(A)}\,{.}\,S
or

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{!!}{\ell(A)}\,{.}\,S
.

An exception-aware runtime typing environment
\Theta
is
output-flat
,
written
{\textsf{flat}}(\Theta)
, if each output-directed choice type in
\Theta
is
output-flat.

We now define a
flattening
of an output choice. A session type is a
flattening of an output sum if it is a unary sum consisting of one of the
choices.

Definition 43

(Flattening (types))
.

A session type
S^{\prime}
is a
flattening of
an output-directed choice

S=\Sigma^{\bullet}_{i\in I}(\alpha_{i}\,{.}\,T_{i})
if
S^{\prime}=\alpha_{j}\,{.}\,T_{j}
for some
j\in I
.

We can extend flattening to environments by flattening all output-directed
choices.

Definition 44

(Flattening (environments))
.

Given an exception-aware runtime typing environment:

\Theta=s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{1}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}_{1}\rangle{:}S^{\bullet}_{1},\ldots,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{l}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}_{l}\rangle{:}S^{\bullet}_{l},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}_{1}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{s}}}}_{1}\rangle{:}T_{1},\ldots,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}_{m}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{s}}}}_{m}\rangle{:}T_{m},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{t}}}_{1}]:\lightning,\ldots,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{t}}}_{n}]:\lightning

where each
T
is not an output-directed choice, we say that
\Theta^{\prime}
is a

flattening
of
\Theta
if:

\Theta=s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{1}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}_{1}\rangle{:}S^{\bullet}{{}^{\prime}}_{1},\ldots,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{l}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}_{l}\rangle{:}S^{\bullet}{{}^{\prime}}_{l},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}_{1}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{s}}}}_{1}\rangle{:}T_{1},\ldots,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}_{m}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{s}}}}_{m}\rangle{:}T_{m},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{t}}}_{1}]:\lightning,\ldots,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{t}}}_{n}]:\lightning

where each
S^{\bullet}{{}^{\prime}}_{i}
is a flattening of
S^{\bullet}_{i}
.

Progress states that however an environment reduces, an active role will be
eventually be able to reduce. In the case that the environment does not reduce,
it is final.

Definition 45

(Ready)
.

A configuration
\mathcal{C}
is
ready
, written
{\textsf{ready}}(\mathcal{C})
,
if all subconfigurations are either zapper threads or actors evaluating terms
of the form
E[M]
where
M
is a communication action.

Lemma 46

(Flattening typability)
.

If
\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{G}[\mathcal{S}]
where
\mathcal{S}=(\nu s:\Theta)\mathcal{C}
and
{\textsf{ready}}(\mathcal{C})
,
then there exists some
\Theta^{\prime}
such that
{\textsf{flat}}(\Theta^{\prime})
where

\Theta^{\prime}
is a flattening of
\Theta
, and

\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{G}[(\nu s:\Theta^{\prime})\mathcal{C}]
.

Proof.

By Lemma
38
, we have that there exist
\Psi,\Delta
such
that
\Delta
does not contain session entries, and

\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{S}
.

By
T-Session
,
\Psi;\Delta,\Theta\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{C}
.

The result follows by induction on the derivation
of
\Psi;\Delta,\Theta\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{C}
. In the case of
T-Zap
,
the result follows immediately.

In the case of
E-Actor
, we have that:

1.

\mathcal{C}=\langle a,M,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}\rangle,\kappa\rangle

2.

\Psi;a:T,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}\rangle{:}S\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{C}

3.

\{T\}\>\>\Psi\,\mid\,{S}\triangleright{M}{:}{A}\triangleleft{{\textsf{end}}}

Since
{\textsf{ready}}(\mathcal{C})
, it must be the case that the actor is evaluating a
term of the form
E[N]
where
N
is a communication action.

By Lemma
25
,
\{T\}\>\>\Psi\,\mid\,{S}\triangleright{N}{:}{B}\triangleleft{S^{\prime}}
for
some
B,S^{\prime}
.

In the case that
N
is a
disconnect
,
wait
,

receive
, or
accept
action, then the session type cannot
be output-directed and the result follows immediately.

In the case that
M={\textbf{{connect}}}\;\ell_{j}(V_{j})\;{\textbf{{to}}}\;{W}\;{\textbf{{as}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}
, by
T-Connect
it must be the case
that
S=\Sigma^{\bullet}_{i\in I}(\alpha_{i}\,{.}\,S^{\prime}_{i})
where

{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}{!!}{\ell_{j}}({A_{j}})\in\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and

{\Psi}\vdash{V_{j}}{:}{A_{j}}
. Again by
T-Connect
and
Lemma
26
, we can show that

\{T\}\>\>\Psi\,\mid\,{{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}{!!}{\ell_{j}}({A_{j}})\,{.}\,S^{\prime}_{i}}\triangleright{E[{\textbf{{connect}}}\;\ell_{j}(V_{j})\;{\textbf{{to}}}\;{W}\;{\textbf{{as}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]}{:}{B}\triangleleft{S^{\prime}}
, where

{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}{!!}{\ell_{j}}({A_{j}})\,{.}\,S^{\prime}_{i}
is a flattening of

S
, as required.

The same argument holds for send actions.
∎

Lemma 47

(Flattening preserves
reducibility)
.

Suppose
{\textsf{safe}}(\Theta)
,
{\textsf{prog}}(\Theta)
, and
\Theta\Longrightarrow
.

If
\hat{\Theta}
is a flattening of
\Theta
, then
\hat{\Theta}\Longrightarrow
.

Proof.

Assume for the sake of contradiction that
\hat{\Theta}\not\Longrightarrow
.

For this to be the case, each
\rho
such that
\Theta\xRightarrow{\rho}
must either be
s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{::}\ell
or

s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\twoheadrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{::}\ell
,
but instead,
\hat{\Theta}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{!}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}^{\prime}{::}\ell^{\prime}(A)}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}^{\prime}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}
.
Note that
\rho
cannot be a disconnection action, since these are
unaffected by flattening, and it cannot be the case that

\hat{\Theta}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\twoheadrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}^{\prime}{::}\ell^{\prime}{A}}
, since this is a
synchronisation action and could reduce.

By
(eventual communication)
, either

\Theta\xRightarrow{\overrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}}\Theta^{\prime}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{?}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}^{\prime}{::}\ell^{\prime}(A)}
or

\Theta\xRightarrow{\overrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}}\Theta^{\prime}\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\lightning}
.

Pick some
\rho
such that
\overrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}
contains no roles affected by
flattening; one such
\rho
must exist since otherwise there would be a
cycle, contradicting progress.

It follows that either:

•

\hat{\Theta}\xRightarrow{\overrightarrow{\rho}}\Theta^{\prime}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{!}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}^{\prime}{::}\ell^{\prime}(A)}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}^{\prime}{?}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{::}\ell^{\prime}(A)}

meaning

\hat{\Theta}\xRightarrow{\overrightarrow{\rho}}\Theta^{\prime}\xRightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}^{\prime}{::}\ell^{\prime}}
, a contradiction; or

•

\Theta\xRightarrow{\overrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}}\Theta^{\prime}\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}^{\prime}:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\lightning}
, also a contradiction.

∎

C.6
Session progress

Lemma 48

(Readiness)
.

Suppose
\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{C}
where

\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{G}[\mathcal{S}]
,

\mathcal{C}
does not contain unmatched discovers,

\mathcal{S}
is not a failed session,

\mathcal{S}=(\nu s:\Theta)\mathcal{D}
, and
{\textsf{prog}}(\Theta)
.

Either
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow
or
{\textsf{ready}}(\mathcal{D})
.

Proof.

For each
\langle a,M,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}\rangle,\kappa\rangle
,
by Lemma
28
,
M
can either reduce or is either a value,
adaptation construct, or communication construct.

•

If
M
can reduce, then the configuration can reduce by

E-LiftM
.

•

If
M=E[{\textbf{{raise}}}]
, then the configuration either can reduce by

E-TryRaise
and
E-LiftM
, or
E-FailS
.

•

If
M
is an adaptation action, due to the absence of unmatched
discovers, the configuration can reduce by
E-New
,

E-Replace
,
E-ReplaceSelf
,
E-Discover
, or

E-Self
.

•

If
M
is a value, then by
T-ConnectedActor
,

\{T\}\>\>\Psi\,\mid\,{{\textsf{end}}}\triangleright{{\textbf{{return}}}\>V}{:}{A}\triangleleft{{\textsf{end}}}
. As a consequence of

{\textsf{prog}}(\Theta)
,
a
must be the only actor in the session and thus the
configuration could reduce by
E-Complete
.

•

It follows that all terms must be evaluating communication actions,
satisfying
{\textsf{ready}}(\mathcal{D})
.

∎

Lemma 49

(Exception-aware session progress)
.

If
\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{C}
where:

1.

\mathcal{C}\equiv\mathcal{G}[\mathcal{S}]
,

2.

\mathcal{S}=(\nu s:\Theta)\mathcal{D}
,

3.

{\textsf{flat}}(\Theta)
,

4.

{\textsf{ready}}(\mathcal{C})
,

5.

\Theta\Longrightarrow

then
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow
.

Proof.

Since
\Theta\Longrightarrow
, there exists some environment
\Theta^{\prime}
and
label
\rho
such that
\Theta\xRightarrow{\rho}\Theta^{\prime}
.

The proof is by induction on the derivation of
\Theta\xRightarrow{\rho}\Theta^{\prime}
.

Case
ET-Conn

\frac{\exists j\in I.S_{j}={{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}{!!}{\ell_{j}}({A_{j}})\,{.}\,S^{\prime}_{j}\quad\quad{\textsf{ty}}({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}})=\Sigma_{k\in K}({{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}}{??}{\ell_{k}}({B_{k}})\,{.}\,T_{k})\quad j\in K\quad A_{j}=B_{j}}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}\rangle{:}\Sigma_{i\in I}(S_{i})\xRightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\twoheadrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{::}\ell_{j}}s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}\rangle{:}S^{\prime}_{j},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]\langle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\rangle{:}T_{j}}

Since
{\textsf{flat}}(\Theta)
, we have that

s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}\rangle{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}{!!}{\ell_{j}}({A_{j}})\,{.}\,S^{\prime}_{j}
. Thus,
\mathcal{C}
must
contain an actor evaluating
E[{\textbf{{connect}}}\;\ell_{j}(A_{j})\;{\textbf{{to}}}\;{V}\;{\textbf{{as}}}\;b]
for some
actor name
b
. Due to the definition of canonical forms, actor
b
must be
a subconfiguration of
\mathcal{G}[\mathcal{S}]
and thus the configuration can
reduce by either
E-Conn
or
E-ConnFail
.

Case
ET-Comm

\frac{\Theta_{1}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{!}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{::}\ell(A)}\Theta^{\prime}_{1}\quad\Theta_{2}\xrightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{?}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{::}\ell(A)}\Theta^{\prime}_{2}}{\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2}\xRightarrow{s{:}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{::}\ell}\Theta^{\prime}_{1},\Theta^{\prime}_{2}}\quad\textsc{ET-Comm}

By
ET-Act
,
\Theta_{1}
must contain

s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}\rangle{:}S
; since
{\textsf{flat}}(\Theta)
,

S={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}{!}{\ell(A)}
.

Also by
ET-Act
,
\Theta_{2}
must contain

s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}\rangle{:}\Sigma_{i\in I}(\beta_{i}\,{.}\,T^{\prime}_{i})
with
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{?}{\ell(A)}\in\{\beta_{i}\}
.

Thus by typing,
\mathcal{C}
must contain an actor

\langle a,E[{\textbf{{send}}}\;\ell(V)\;{\textbf{{to}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}],s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}\rangle,\kappa_{a}\rangle
,
and another actor

\langle b,E^{\prime}[{\textbf{{receive}}}\;{\textbf{{from}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\;\{\ell_{i}(x_{i})\mapsto M_{i}\}],s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{s}}}}\rangle,\kappa_{b}\rangle
, which would
reduce by
E-Comm
.

Case
ET-CommFail

\frac{\exists j\in I.S_{j}={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}{\dagger}{\ell_{j}(A_{j})}\,{.}\,S^{\prime}_{j}}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]:\lightning,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}\rangle{:}\Sigma_{i\in I}(\alpha_{i}\,{.}\,S^{\prime}_{i})\xRightarrow{s:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\lightning{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]:\lightning,s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]:\lightning}\quad\textsc{ET-CommFail}

We prove the case where
\dagger={!}
. Since
{\textsf{flat}}(\Theta)
, by typing we have
that
\Sigma_{i\in I}(\alpha_{i}\,{.}\,S^{\prime}_{i})={\ell}{!}{A}({{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}})\,{.}\,S^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{C}
must contain an actor

\langle a,E[{\textbf{{send}}}\;\ell(V)\;{\textbf{{to}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}],s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{r}}}}\rangle,\kappa_{a}\rangle

and a zapper thread
\lightning s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}]
, which could then reduce by

E-RaiseP
.

Case
E-Disconn
is similar to
E-Conn
, and cases

E-DisconnFail
and
E-WaitFail
are similar to
E-CommFail
.

Cases
ET-Rec
,
ET-Cong1
, and
ET-Cong2
follow by the
induction hypothesis.
∎

Lemma 50
.

If
{\textsf{prog}}(\Theta)
and neither
{\textsf{end}}(\Theta)
nor
{\textsf{failed}}(\Theta)
,
then
\Theta\Longrightarrow
.

Proof.

By contradiction. Assume that
{\textsf{prog}}(\Theta)
and neither

{\textsf{end}}(\Theta)
nor
{\textsf{failed}}(\Theta)
.

By
{\textsf{prog}}(\Theta)
, each active role must eventually reduce.

If no roles are active, then
{\textsf{end}}(\Theta)
or
{\textsf{failed}}(\Theta)
: a
contradiction.

Otherwise, by the definition of
{\textsf{prog}}(\Theta)

for each
s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}\rangle{:}S

there must exist some
\overrightarrow{\rho}
such that
\Theta\xRightarrow{\overrightarrow{\rho^{\prime}}}{}^{*}\Theta^{\prime}\xRightarrow{\rho}
where
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}\in{\textsf{subj}}(\rho)
: a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 51
.

If
\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{\mathcal{S}}
where
\mathcal{S}=(\nu s:\Theta)\mathcal{C}
and
{\textsf{ready}}(\mathcal{C})
, then
\Theta
is not final.

Proof.

By contradiction. If
\Theta
were final, it would consist only of session
types of type
end
. No communication actions are typable under

end
, contradicting
{\textsf{ready}}(\mathcal{C})
.
∎

See
18

Proof.

•

By Lemma
29
,
\exists.\Psi,\Delta^{\prime}
such that

\Psi;\Delta^{\prime}\vdash\mathcal{S}
.

•

By definition,
\mathcal{S}=(\nu s:\Delta)\mathcal{D}
,
where
\mathcal{D}=\langle a_{1},M_{1},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{1}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}_{1}\rangle,\kappa_{1}\rangle\parallel\cdots\parallel\langle a_{m},M_{m},s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{m}]\langle\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{q}}}}_{m}\rangle,\kappa_{m}\rangle\parallel{\rm ZAp}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{m+1}]}\parallel\cdots\parallel{\rm ZAp}{s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{n}]}
.

•

By
T-Session
,
{\textsf{safe}}(\Delta)
.

•

By definition, the zapped roles of
\mathcal{C}
are
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{m+1},\ldots,{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{n}
. Let us denote this set as
\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}}
.

•

Let
\Theta={\textsf{zap}}(\Delta,\tilde{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}})
.

•

By Lemma
36
,
\Psi;\Delta^{\prime},\Theta\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{D}

and so by Corollary
37
,

\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{G}[\mathcal{S}]
.

•

By Lemma
40
,
{\textsf{safe}}(\Theta)
and

{\textsf{prog}}(\Theta)
.

•

By Lemma
48
, either
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow
or

{\textsf{ready}}(\mathcal{D})
. As
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow
satisfies the theorem
statement, we proceed assuming
{\textsf{ready}}(\mathcal{D})
.

•

By Lemma
46
, there exists some
\hat{\Theta}
such
that
\hat{\Theta}
is a flattening of
\Theta
and

\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\lightning}\mathcal{G}[(\nu s:\hat{\Theta})\mathcal{D}]
.

•

By Lemma
51
,
\hat{\Theta}
is not final.

•

By Lemma
47
,
\hat{\Theta}\Longrightarrow
.

•

Thus, by Lemma
49
,
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow
as
required.

∎

C.7
Progress

See
19

Proof.

•

By Lemma
31
, write
\mathcal{C}
in
canonical form.

•

Eliminate all failed sessions via the equivalence
(\nu s)({\rm ZAp}{s}{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{1}}\parallel\cdots\parallel\lightning s[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\mathttbf{p}}}_{n}])\parallel\mathcal{C}\equiv\mathcal{C}
. Heneceforth assume no sessions are failed.

•

By repeated application of Lemma
18
, either

\mathcal{C}
can reduce or it contains no sessions.

•

Thus we only need to consider disconnected actors. For each disconnected
actor
\langle a,M,\bot,\kappa\rangle
, by Lemma
28
,
either:

1.

M={\textbf{{return}}}\>V
. If
\kappa=N
, then the configuration
can reduce by
E-Loop
. Otherwise, if
\kappa={{\textbf{{stop}}}}
,
then the actor is terminated, as required.

2.

M=E[{\textbf{{raise}}}]
.
If
E
contains an exception handler, the configuration can reduce by

E-TryRaise
. If not,
E
is pure: if
\kappa=N
, then
the configuration can reduce by
E-FailLoop
. Otherwise, if

\kappa={{\textbf{{stop}}}}
, then the actor is terminated, as required.

3.

M=E[N]
where
N
is a communication or concurrency action,
which, following the logic in Lemma
18
, can
either reduce by a configuration reduction rule or is a communication
action.
Since:

–

by
T-UnconnectedActor
each disconnected actor must
either have type
end
or be following its statically-defined
session type

–

by well-formedness, each statically-defined session type must
correspond to a role in a protocol

–

by well-formedness, each protocol must have a unique initiator

–

since the program satisfies progress, all protocols satisfy progress

it must be the case that either
a
is an initiator of a session and
blocked on
connect
, which could either reduce by

E-ConnInit
or
E-ConnFail
, or be accepting, as
required.

∎
